<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:0pt'>The Midstream business segment reported a slight decrease of Ps. 2,835 million, mainly explained by the negative impact of the exchange rate measured in homogeneous currency, an effect that was partially offset by higher natural gas transportation and conditioning services in Vaca Muerta, as well as operation and maintenance services.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:0pt'>Cost of sales and administrative and commercialization expenses for 4Q2024 amounted to Ps. 192,078 million (compared to Ps. 173,237 million in 4Q2023), which represented an increase of Ps. 18,841 million. This variation is mainly due to higher: (i) expenses for asset repair and maintenance, (ii) cost of natural gas processed at the Cerri Complex (higher consumption, partially offset by the lower price measured in homogeneous currency), and (iii) taxes, fees, and contributions (increase in gross income and withholdings due to higher sales revenue) and third-party fees and services.</P>

<P align=justify style='font:11pt Verdana;margin-top:12pt;margin-bottom:3pt;margin-right:-24.75pt;color:#0070C0'><B>9. Board of Directors&#8217; Proposal regarding the application of Results</B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:10pt Verdana;margin:0'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0'>The chart below provides a breakdown of our retained earnings as of December 31, 2024 and our proposal for its distribution. Our proposal includes, after the reversal of the Balance of the former &#8220;Reserve for Future Investments, Buyback of our Own Shares and/or Payment of Dividends&#8221; constituted by <B>tgs</B> Ordinary Shareholders Meeting dated April 17, 2024, the creation of a further &#8220;Reserve for Future Investments, Buyback of our Own Shares and/or Payment of Dividends&#8221;. In light of our new businesses outlook and the challenges we are facing in this oncoming fiscal year, it might be destined to:  (i) future investmens related to the projects and businesses outlined in the Company&#8217;s business plan and and/or (ii) the payment of future dividends subject to the financial condition of the Company and the macroenconomic variables and/or (iii) the buyback of shares within the plan approved by the Board of Directors of the Company. &nbsp;The mentioned reserve might be fully or partially distributed by the Board of Directors &#8212;as long as its use is not required in accordance with the provisions of subsections c) and e) of item 11, section 3, Chapter III Title IV of the CNV Standards (N.T. 2013), which with reference to treasury stock, based on the destination, opportunity and amount determined by the Board and approved by the Shareholders Meeting , in line with a reasonable management &nbsp;criteria as outlined by applicable regulations and considering for such purposes, the economic &nbsp;financial condition of the Company and its future outlook.<FONT style='font-family:Book Antiqua'> &#160;</FONT></P>

<HR style='border:0;height:0;width:0;margin:14pt 0 0 0'><P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-right:28.3pt;color:#0070C0'><KBD style='position:absolute;font:9pt Verdana;margin-left:0pt'><B>1.</B></KBD><KBD style=margin-left:35.4pt></KBD><B>BUSINESS DESCRIPTION</B>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:12pt Times New Roman;margin:0;margin-right:28.3pt;color:#0070C0'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt;color:#0070C0'><B>Business Overview</B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:12pt Times New Roman;margin:0;text-indent:14.2pt;margin-right:28.3pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>Transportadora de Gas del Sur S.A. (&#8220;<B>tgs</B>&#8221; or the &#8220;Company&#8221;) is one of the companies created as a result of the privatization of Gas del Estado S.E. (&#8220;GdE&#8221;). <B>tgs</B> commenced operations on December 28, 1992 and it is mainly engaged in the Transportation of Natural Gas, and Production and Commercialization of natural gas Liquids (&#8220;Liquids&#8221;). TGS&#8217;s pipeline system connects major natural gas fields in southern and western Argentina with natural gas distributors and industries in those areas and in the greater Buenos Aires area. The natural gas transportation license to operate this system was exclusively granted to <B>tgs</B> for a period of thirty-five years (&#8220;the License&#8221;). <B>tgs</B> is entitled to a one-time extension of ten years provided that it has essentially met the obligations imposed by the License and by <I>Ente Nacional Regulador del Gas</I> (National Gas Regulatory Body or &#8220;ENARGAS&#8221;). The General Cerri Gas Processing Complex (the &#8220;Cerri Complex&#8221;), where TGS processes natural gas to obtain liquids, was transferred from GdE along with the gas transmission assets. <B>tgs</B> also provides midstream services, which mainly consist of gas treatment, removal of impurities from the natural gas stream, gas compression, wellhead gas gathering and pipeline construction, operation and maintenance services. In addition, telecommunications services are provided through the subsidiary Telcosur S.A. (&#8220;Telcosur&#8221;). These services consist of data transmission services through a network of terrestrial and digital radio relay.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>Subsequently, the corporate purpose of the Company was modified to incorporate the development of complementary activities, incidental, linked and / or derived from natural gas transportation, such as the generation and commercialization of electric power and the provision of other services for the hydrocarbon sector in general.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt;color:#0070C0'><B>Dedicated Branch</B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt;color:#0070C0'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>On November 4, 2024, the Board of Directors of the Company decided to establish the Dedicated Branch Transportadora de Gas del Sur S.A. &#8211;&nbsp;Sucursal Dedicada 1 (hereinafter &#8220;Dedicated Branch&#8221;), to request adherence to the Large Investment Incentive Regime (&#8220;RIGI&#8221;), in relation to the project to expand the transportation capacity of the Perito Francisco Pascasio Moreno Gas Pipeline (&#8220;Perito Moreno Gas Pipeline&#8221;), on the Tratay&#233;n &#8211;&nbsp;Salliquel&#243; route.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>The RIGI promotes investment in projects that qualify as large long-term investments in Argentina by granting a series of tax, customs, and exchange incentives, as well as an efficient system for the protection of rights and resolution of disputes. Projects that adhere to the RIGI must be developed in specific sectors defined by Law 27,742 and Decree 749/2024, including the activities of Transportation and Storage of oil and gas.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>The Dedicated Branch will have the sole purpose of executing of a unique project in the oil and gas sector, which involves transportation capacity of section I of the Perito Moreno Gas Pipeline, on the Tratay&#233;n - Saliquell&#243; route and the exploitation of the project. This includes transporting, using and/or disposing of the natural gas transportation capacity resulting from the expansion project for itself or third parties through any title, modality or figure permitted by applicable regulations. For such purposes, the Dedicated Branch may carry out all those activities that are accessory, complementary and/or subsidiary to the execution, operation and maintenance of said project (including but not limited to the construction, operation and/or maintenance of pipelines, facilities and/or plants) and the transportation and/or disposal of hydrocarbon transportation capacity under the terms permitted by applicable laws; having full legal capacity to acquire rights and/or enter into obligations and/or perform all acts that are not prohibited by law or its bylaws (including fulfilling mandates and commissions). To achieve its purpose, it may also carry out any type of financial operations in general, excluding those provided for in the Financial Institutions Act.</P>

<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt;color:#0070C0'><B>Major Shareholders</B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'><B>tgs&#180; </B>controlling shareholder Compa&#241;&#237;a de Inversiones de Energ&#237;a S.A. (&#8220;CIESA&#8221;), holds 51% of the common stock of the company, the National Social Security Administration (&#8220;ANSES&#8221;) holds 24% and the remaining 25% is held by the investing public (<B>tgs</B> has 5.25% of the shares in the portfolio).</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>CIESA is under joint control of Pampa Energ&#237;a S.A. (&#8220;Pampa Energ&#237;a&#8221;) with 50% and Grupo Inversor Petroqu&#237;mica S.L. (&#8220;GIP&#8221;) and PCT L.L.C. with the remaining 50%. </P>

<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt;color:#0070C0'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>The Company operates in a complex economic context whose main variables have recently had strong volatility as a result of political and economic events at the national level.</P>

<P align=center style='font:8pt Verdana;margin:0;text-indent:-1.15pt;margin-right:-35.5pt'>AS OF DECEMBER 31, 2024 AND COMPARATIVE INFORMATION </P>
<P align=center style='font:8pt Verdana;margin:0;text-indent:-1.15pt;margin-right:-35.5pt'>(Stated in thousands of pesos as described in Note 3, unless otherwise stated)</P>
<P align=center style='font:8pt Times New Roman;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt'>_____________________________________________________________________________________________________</P>
<HR style='border:0;height:0;width:0;margin:14pt 0 0 0'><P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>Among the main macroeconomic variables for 2024 we can mention:</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt'><KBD style='position:absolute;font:9pt Symbol;margin-left:0pt'>&#183;</KBD><KBD style=margin-left:35.4pt></KBD>The CPI for 2024 was 117.8%, while the CPI for 2023 was 211.4%. After taking office on December 10, 2023 and raising the official exchange rate from $366 to $800, inflation for the month of December 2023 registered a variation of 25.5%, the highest in the last 3 decades. In 2024, meanwhile, after increases of 20.6%, 13.2% and 11% were recorded in the first 3 months of the year, the speed of inflation slowed down to 2.7% in October, the lowest figure in 3 years.&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:50.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt'><KBD style='position:absolute;font:9pt Symbol;margin-left:0pt'>&#183;</KBD><KBD style=margin-left:35.4pt></KBD>The IPIM variation for the year 2024 was 67.1%, having accumulated 276.4% in 2023.&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt'><KBD style='position:absolute;font:9pt Symbol;margin-left:0pt'>&#183;</KBD><KBD style=margin-left:35.4pt></KBD>Following the aforementioned devaluation of the Argentine peso on December 12, 2023, the Argentine government defined a stable devaluation path of 2% per month for the US currency. The exchange rate, as published by the BNA, ended 2024 at $ 1,032.00 per US dollar, while as of December 31, 2023, it was $ 808.45 at the end of 2023 (representing an increase of approximately 27.7%, below the inflation measured by the CPI). Regarding the exchange rate gap, at the time of the current administration's assumption of office, it was above 150%, while at the end of 2024 it was 13% approximately.&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:50.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt'><KBD style='position:absolute;font:9pt Symbol;margin-left:0pt'>&#183;</KBD><KBD style=margin-left:35.4pt></KBD>The risk premium paid on Argentine debt, measured by the EMBI+ prepared by JP Morgan, stood at around 1,906 points as of December 31, 2023. As of December 31, 2024, it was 635 points.&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:50.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt'><KBD style='position:absolute;font:9pt Symbol;margin-left:0pt'>&#183;</KBD><KBD style=margin-left:35.4pt></KBD>Economic activity: GDP showed a decline of 2.1% in the third quarter of 2024, compared to the same period of the previous year. Meanwhile, in November 2024, the monthly economic activity estimator (&#8220;EMAE&#8221;) recorded a year-on-year decline of 0.9%. During 2024, the performance of this indicator was somewhat uneven and heterogeneous depending on each sector of the economy. While energy (favored by the development of Vaca Muerta) and mining showed the most significant increases, construction, industry and commerce fell compared to 2023.&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:50.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt'><KBD style='position:absolute;font:9pt Symbol;margin-left:0pt'>&#183;</KBD><KBD style=margin-left:35.4pt></KBD>The December 2024 Wage Index recorded a year-on-year increase of 145.5%. It is important to highlight the disparity shown by the wage evolution of private workers, compared to state workers and unregistered wages. Something similar has occurred with the unemployment rate, which rose to 6.9% in the third quarter of 2024.&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:50.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt'><KBD style='position:absolute;font:9pt Symbol;margin-left:0pt'>&#183;</KBD><KBD style=margin-left:35.4pt></KBD>BCRA reserves remained under pressure despite measures implemented to accelerate the inflow of foreign currency. As of December 31, 2024, net reserves reached US$29,612 million, from US$23,073 million in December 2023.&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:50.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt'><KBD style='position:absolute;font:9pt Symbol;margin-left:0pt'>&#183;</KBD><KBD style=margin-left:35.4pt></KBD>The trade balance for 2024 was in surplus by US$ 18,899 million,<FONT style='font-family:Times New Roman'> </FONT>having been in deficit by US$ 6,925 million in 2023. This was mainly driven bythe increase in agricultural and energy exports and the decrease in imports.&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:50.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt'><KBD style='position:absolute;font:9pt Symbol;margin-left:0pt'>&#183;</KBD><KBD style=margin-left:35.4pt></KBD>Since the first month of management, Javier Milei's government has managed to reverse the fiscal deficit, achieving for the first time in 14 years a primary and financial surplus. In this regard, the financial surplus was $1.8 trillion, equivalent to 0.3% of GDP. Meanwhile, the primary surplus reached 1.8% of GDP. This was achieved thanks to a 27% reduction in primary expenditures. &nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>On December 10, 2023, a new administration took office, marking a change in the economic, political, and international direction the country was taking. At the time of its inauguration, unfavorable macroeconomic conditions and uncertainty regarding the political governability of the new administration led to the adoption of a series of measures within the framework of an emergency scheme aimed at eliminating the fiscal deficit (via a strong fiscal adjustment), reducing monetary issuance to zero by the BCRA (with the consequent drop in inflation), and readjusting relative prices, especially for public services.</P>
<HR style='border:0;height:0;width:0;margin:14pt 0 0 0'><P align=center style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0'>7</P>
<HR style='page-break-after:always;border:0;height:3pt;background-color:#909090;margin:8pt 0'><P style=line-height:0;margin:0></P>
<P align=right style='font:12pt Times New Roman;margin:0;margin-right:-21.3pt'><B><IMG src=tgs_86.jpg width=156 height=55 alt='Picture 4' title='Picture 4'></B>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=center style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:0pt'>English translation of the original prepared in Spanish for publication in Argentina</P>
<P align=center style='font:8pt Verdana;margin:0'><B>TRANSPORTADORA DE GAS DEL SUR S.A.</B></P>
<P align=center style='font:8pt Verdana;margin:0;text-indent:-1.15pt;margin-right:-35.5pt'>NOTES TO THE CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS </P>
<P align=center style='font:8pt Verdana;margin:0;text-indent:-1.15pt;margin-right:-35.5pt'>AS OF DECEMBER 31, 2024 AND COMPARATIVE INFORMATION </P>
<P align=center style='font:8pt Verdana;margin:0;text-indent:-1.15pt;margin-right:-35.5pt'>(Stated in thousands of pesos as described in Note 3, unless otherwise stated)</P>
<P align=center style='font:8pt Times New Roman;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt'>_____________________________________________________________________________________________________</P>
<HR style='border:0;height:0;width:0;margin:14pt 0 0 0'><P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>Along with these measures, the government formalized a series of reforms, including the decree deregulating the Argentine state, the tax law and perhaps the most relevant of all, the submission to the National Congress of the text of the <I>Ley Bases</I>. After six months of debate in the National Congress, on June 28, 2024, the Chamber of Deputies approved the final text of the Basic Law, which had previously received partial approval from the Senate. Among other issues, the Basic Law includes:</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt'><KBD style='position:absolute;font:9pt Symbol;margin-left:0pt'>&#183;</KBD><KBD style=margin-left:35.4pt></KBD>The declaration of public emergency in administrative, economic, financial and energy matters for a period of one year.&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt'><KBD style='position:absolute;font:9pt Symbol;margin-left:0pt'>&#183;</KBD><KBD style=margin-left:35.4pt></KBD>Changes in the calculation of income tax for individuals, in the single tax regime, personal property and money laundering.&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt'><KBD style='position:absolute;font:9pt Symbol;margin-left:0pt'>&#183;</KBD><KBD style=margin-left:35.4pt></KBD>The privatization, whether total or partial, of certain companies and corporations that are wholly or majority owned by the national State is authorized.&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt'><KBD style='position:absolute;font:9pt Symbol;margin-left:0pt'>&#183;</KBD><KBD style=margin-left:35.4pt></KBD>A Large Investment Incentive Regime (&#8220;RIGI&#8221;) for projects that meet certain conditions. The RIGI is a regime that grants tax, customs and exchange benefits with the aim of increasing the level of private investment in the economy.&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt'><KBD style='position:absolute;font:9pt Symbol;margin-left:0pt'>&#183;</KBD><KBD style=margin-left:35.4pt></KBD>A reform of the labor and retirement system.&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt'><KBD style='position:absolute;font:9pt Symbol;margin-left:0pt'>&#183;</KBD><KBD style=margin-left:35.4pt></KBD>The amendment to the Natural Gas Law N&#176; 24,076 in order to, among other things, allow the extension of the License for an additional period of 20 years (as opposed to the 10-year extension originally established).&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt'><KBD style='position:absolute;font:9pt Symbol;margin-left:0pt'>&#183;</KBD><KBD style=margin-left:35.4pt></KBD>Amendments to Hydrocarbons Law N&#176; 17,319 to provide legal certainty for Argentinas&#180; energy development and ensure free trade and competition.&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>The <I>Ley Bases</I> has been challenged by various sectors of the economy and the political spectrum and has been challenged in court, however, this is of utmost importance for the economic and institutional restructuring and recovery of the country.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>Although some of the reforms proposed by the <I>Ley Bases</I> have already been implemented, others are in the process of being regulated and discussed by the legislature. For this reason, at the date of issue of the Report, it is not possible to foresee the impact that the <I>Ley Bases</I> and the fiscal package could have on the results, financial situation and cash flow of the Company. However, given the financial situation of <B>tgs</B>, it is estimated that it will be able to continue to meet its financial commitments in the near future.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>The context of volatility and uncertainty continues at the date of issue of these consolidated financial statements. Government measures already implemented, or those that may be implemented in the future, could eventually affect the results of operations, financial situation and assets of the Company.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>Likewise, the Company's management permanently monitors the evolution of the variables that affect its business, to define its course of action and identify the potential impacts on its equity and financial situation. The reforms proposed by the new government began their legislative discussion process. It is not possible to predict at this time its evolution or new measures that could be announced. Likewise, the Company cannot guarantee that the aforementioned macroeconomic difficulties or the adoption of new measures by the Argentine Government to control inflation may affect its operations and financial situation.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>The Company's financial statements must be read in light of these circumstances.</P>

<P align=justify style='font:12pt Times New Roman;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>

<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>Its consolidated financial statements including Telcosur S.A. (&#8220;Telcosur&#8221;) and CTG Energ&#237;a S.A.U. (Liquidated) (&#8220;CTG&#8221;) its consolidated subsidiaries, which are jointly referred to as &#8220;<B>tgs</B>&#8221; or &#8220;the Company&#8221;.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>For more information on the liquidation of the subsidiary CTG, see Note 4.b.1) &#8220;Significant Accounting Policies &#8211;&nbsp;Subsidiaries&#8221; to these consolidated financial statements.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:2.55pt'>These consolidated financial statements were approved and authorized for issuance by the Company&#180;s Board of Directors on February 27, 2025.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:12pt Times New Roman;margin:0;margin-left:35.2pt;margin-right:28.3pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:12pt Times New Roman;margin:0;margin-left:35.2pt;margin-right:28.3pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-right:28.3pt;color:#0070C0'><KBD style='position:absolute;font:9pt Verdana;margin-left:0pt'><B>3.</B></KBD><KBD style=margin-left:35.4pt></KBD><B>BASIS OF PRESENTATION</B>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:12pt Times New Roman;margin:0;margin-left:35.2pt;margin-right:28.3pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>These consolidated financial statements have been prepared in accordance with International Financial Reporting Standards as issued by the International Accounting Standards Board (&#8220;IASB&#8221;) and the International Financial Interpretations Committee (&#8220;IFRIC&#8221;), jointly the &#8220;IFRS Accounting Standards&#8221;.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>The CNV, in Title IV, Chapter III, Article 1 of the Rules has provided that listed companies must submit their consolidated financial statements by applying Technical Resolution No. 26 amended by Resolution N&#176; 29 of the Argentine Federation of Professional Councils of Economic Sciences (&quot;FACPCE&quot;), which adopts the IFRS Accounting Standards issued by the IASB, its amendments and circulars for the adoption of IFRS Accounting Standards that the FACPCE dictates in accordance with the provisions of that Technical Resolution.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>The preparation of the consolidated financial statements in conformity with IFRS Accounting Standards requires management to make accounting estimates and assumptions that affect the reported amounts of assets and liabilities and the disclosure of contingent assets and liabilities at the date of the consolidated financial statements, as well as the reported amounts of revenues and expenses during the reporting fiscal year. Estimates are used when accounting for the allowance for doubtful accounts, income taxes, provisions for legal claims and others, depreciations and recoverable value of assets. Actual results could be significantly different from such estimates.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>The presentation in the statement of financial position distinguishes between current and non-current assets and liabilities. The assets and liabilities are those expected to be realized or settled within twelve months after the end of the reporting period under review, and those held for sale. The fiscal year begins on January 1 and ends on December 31 of each year. The economic and financial results are presented on a fiscal year basis. </P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>The consolidated financial statements have been stated in thousands of Argentine pesos (&#8220;Ps.&#8221;) which is the functional currency of the Company, unless otherwise stated, and restated to reflect the effects of inflation as indicated in Note 4.d).</P>

<P align=center style='font:8pt Times New Roman;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt'>_____________________________________________________________________________________________________</P>
<HR style='border:0;height:0;width:0;margin:14pt 0 0 0'><P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt;color:#0070C0'><KBD style='position:absolute;font:9pt Verdana;margin-left:0pt'><B>4.</B></KBD><KBD style=margin-left:35.4pt></KBD><B>SIGNIFICANT ACCOUNTING POLICIES</B>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:12pt Times New Roman;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;color:#0070C1'><B>4.a) New IFRS accounting standards</B></P>
<P style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;color:#0070C1'>&nbsp;</P>
<P style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;color:#0070C1'><B>4.a 1) New standards and interpretations issued by the IASB effective for the years</B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'><FONT style=color:#0070C1><B>beginning on or after January 1, 2024 adopted by the Company</B></FONT></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>The new accounting standards, amendments and interpretations issued by the IASB that became effective as of January 1, 2024 and that have not had an impact on the Company&#8217;s consolidated financial statements are the following:</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C1'><B>Supplier finance arrangements &#8211;&nbsp;Amendments to IAS 7 &#8220;Statement of cash flows&#8221; and IFRS Accounting standard 7 &#8220;Financial instruments &#8211;&nbsp;Disclosure&#8221;</B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;text-indent:14.2pt;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#000000'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>In May 2023, the IASB has issued new reporting requirements on supplier financing arrangements. The modifications include new requirements for qualitative and quantitative information regarding financing agreements with financial providers that allow evaluating the effects of these agreements on liabilities, cash flows and exposure to liquidity risk.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;text-indent:28.35pt;margin-left:14.2pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>Supplier financing arrangements are those characterized by: (i) a financial provider paying the amount that an entity owes to its commercial supplier and (ii) the entity pays the financial provider, according to the terms of the agreement.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>The application of this accounting standard did not have a significant impact on the Company&#180;s consolidated financial statements.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C1'><B>Amendments to IAS 1 - Classification of Liabilities as Current or Non-current and Non-current liabilities with covenants</B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C1'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>Amendments made to IAS 1 clarify that liabilities are classified as either current or non-current, depending on the rights that exist at the end of the reporting period. Classification is unaffected by the entity&#8217;s expectations or events after the reporting date (for example, the receipt of a waiver or a breach of covenant that an entity is required to comply with only after the reporting period).</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>Covenants of loan arrangements will not affect classification of a liability as current or non-current at the reporting date if the entity must only comply with the covenants after the reporting date. However, if the entity must comply with a covenant either on or before the reporting date, this needs to be considered in the classification as current or non-current even if the covenant is only tested for compliance after the reporting date.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>The application of this accounting standard did not have significant impact on the Company&#180;s consolidated financial statements.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C1'><B>Amendments to IFRS Accounting standard 16 &#8211;&nbsp;Lease liability in sale and leaseback</B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C1'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>In September 2022, the IASB finalized narrow-scope amendments to the requirements for sale and leaseback transactions in IFRS Accounting standard 16 - Leases which explain how an entity accounts for a sale and leaseback after the date of the transaction.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>The amendments specify that, in measuring the lease liability subsequent to the sale and leaseback, the seller-lessee determines &#8216;lease payments&#8217; and &#8216;revised lease payments&#8217; in a way that does not result in the seller-lessee recognizing any amount of the gain or loss that relates to the right of use that it retains. This could particularly impact sale and leaseback transactions where the lease payments include variable payments that do not depend on an index or a rate.</P>
<HR style='border:0;height:0;width:0;margin:14pt 0 0 0'><P align=center style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0'>10</P>
<HR style='page-break-after:always;border:0;height:3pt;background-color:#909090;margin:8pt 0'><P style=line-height:0;margin:0></P>
<P align=right style='font:12pt Times New Roman;margin:0;margin-right:-21.3pt'><B><IMG src=tgs_86.jpg width=156 height=55 alt='Picture 4' title='Picture 4'></B>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=center style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:0pt'>English translation of the original prepared in Spanish for publication in Argentina</P>

<P align=center style='font:8pt Verdana;margin:0;text-indent:-1.15pt;margin-right:-35.5pt'>NOTES TO THE CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS </P>

<HR style='border:0;height:0;width:0;margin:14pt 0 0 0'><P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>The amendments are applicable for annual periods beginning on or after January 1, 2024. Early application is permitted. Allows application to transactions made after the date of initial application of the amendment.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>The application of this accounting standard did not have significant impact on the Company&#180;s consolidated financial statements.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C1'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C1'><B>4.a.2) New IFRS Accounting standards issued that are not yet effective for the year beginning January 1, 2024.</B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#000000'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#000000'>The IFRS accounting standards that potentially have an impact on the Company, which are not mandatory and have not been adopted early in the year beginning January 1, 2024, are listed below.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#000000'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>On August 15, 2023, CNV General Resolution No. 972/2023 was published in the BO, which indicates that the early application of IFRS and/or its modifications will not be admitted, unless the CNV specifically admits it.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C1'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'><B>IAS 21 &#8220;Effects of variations in foreign currency exchange rates&#8221; &#8211;&nbsp;Lack of exchangeability</B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;text-indent:28.35pt;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>On August 15, 2023, the IASB issued the modification to IAS 21 called &#8220;Lack of exchangeability&#8221; to provide guidelines in the latter case. The amendment requires entities to apply a consistent approach to evaluating whether a currency can be exchanged for another currency, and if not, determining the exchange rate to use for measurement purposes and the disclosures that need to be provided in its financial statements.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>Once the absence of exchangeability between currencies has been identified, the exchange rate must be estimated that represents that which would be obtained in an orderly transaction between market participants and that reflects economic conditions. These modifications do not specify a methodology for estimating the exchange rate to be used, but rather it must be developed by each entity.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>The amendments are effective for annual reporting periods beginning on or after January 1, 2025. Early adoption is permitted. </P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'><B>IFRS Accounting Standard 18 - Presentation and Disclosure in Financial Statements</B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>This is the new standard on presentation and disclosure in financial statements, which replaces IAS 1, with a focus on updates to the statement of profit or loss.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;text-indent:28.35pt;margin-left:14.2pt'>The key new concepts introduced in IFRS Accounting Standard 18 relate to:</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;text-indent:28.35pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:78.55pt'><KBD style='position:absolute;font:9pt Symbol;margin-left:-18pt'>&#183;</KBD>the structure of the statement of profit or loss with defined subtotals;&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:78.55pt'><KBD style='position:absolute;font:9pt Symbol;margin-left:-18pt'>&#183;</KBD>requirement to determine the most useful structure summary for presenting expenses in the statement of profit or loss&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:78.55pt'><KBD style='position:absolute;font:9pt Symbol;margin-left:-18pt'>&#183;</KBD>required disclosures in a single note within the financial statements for certain profit or loss performance measures that are reported outside an entity&#8217;s financial statements (that is, management-defined performance measures); and&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:78.55pt'><KBD style='position:absolute;font:9pt Symbol;margin-left:-18pt'>&#183;</KBD>enhanced principles on aggregation and disaggregation which apply to the primary financial statements and notes in general&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;text-indent:28.35pt;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C1'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>The amendments are effective for annual reporting periods beginning on or after January 1, 2027. Early adoption is permitted. </P>
<HR style='border:0;height:0;width:0;margin:14pt 0 0 0'><P align=center style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0'>11</P>

<P align=center style='font:8pt Times New Roman;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt'>_____________________________________________________________________________________________________</P>
<HR style='border:0;height:0;width:0;margin:14pt 0 0 0'><P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'><B>IFRS Accounting Standard 19 - Subsidiaries without Public Accountability: Disclosures</B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>IFRS Accounting Standard 19, published by the IASB, focuses on financial reporting by non-public interest subsidiaries. This standard allows these subsidiaries to disclose reduced information compared to other IFRSs, which simplifies the preparation of their financial statements and reduces costs, while maintaining the usefulness of the information for users.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;text-indent:28.35pt;margin-left:14.2pt'>A subsidiary is eligible if:</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:78.55pt'><KBD style='position:absolute;font:9pt Symbol;margin-left:-18pt'>&#183;</KBD>it does not have public accountability; and&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:78.55pt'><KBD style='position:absolute;font:9pt Symbol;margin-left:-18pt'>&#183;</KBD>it has an ultimate or intermediate parent that produces consolidated financial statements available for public use that comply with IFRS Accounting Standards.&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;text-indent:28.35pt;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C1'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>The amendments are effective for annual reporting periods beginning on or after January 1, 2027. Early adoption is permitted. </P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'><B>Amendments to IFRS Accounting Standards 9 and 7 - Classification and measurement of financial instruments</B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>On 30 May 2024, the IASB issued targeted amendments to IFRS 9 Financial Instruments and IFRS 7 Financial Instruments: Disclosures to respond to recent questions arising in practice, and to include new requirements not only for financial institutions but also for corporate entities. These amendments: (i) clarify the date of recognition and derecognition of some financial assets and liabilities, with a new exception for some financial liabilities settled through an electronic cash transfer system; (ii) clarify and add further guidance for assessing whether a financial asset meets the solely payments of principal and interest (SPPI) criterion; (iii) add new disclosures for certain instruments with contractual terms that can change cash flows (such as some financial instruments with features linked to the achievement of environment, social and governance targets); and (iv) update the disclosures for equity instruments designated at fair value through other comprehensive income (FVOCI).</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-left:14.2pt'>The amendments are effective for annual reporting periods beginning on or after January 1, 2026. Early adoption is permitted.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;text-indent:28.35pt;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C1'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt;color:#0070C0'><B>4.b) Consolidation</B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt;color:#0070C0'><B>4.b.1) Subsidiaries</B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>Subsidiaries are all entities over which the Company has control. The Company controls an entity when the Company is exposed to, or has rights to, variable returns from its involvement and has the ability to affect those returns through its power over the entity. Subsidiaries are fully consolidated from the date on which control is transferred to the group. They are deconsolidated from the date that control ceases. For this purpose and unless there are specific requirements, it is generally considered that <B>tgs</B> has control, when it has a participation greater than 50% of the available voting rights.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>The accounting policies of the subsidiaries are consistent with the accounting policies adopted by the Company.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>Inter-company transactions, balances and profit or loss from transactions between group companies are eliminated. Unrealized profit or loss are also eliminated. </P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>Detailed data reflecting subsidiary control as of December 31, 2024 is as follows:</P>

<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt;color:#0070C0'><B>4.b.2) Associates</B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>Associates are entities over which the group has significant influence but not control, generally accompanying a shareholding of between 20% and 50% of the voting rights. Investments in associates are accounted for using the equity method of accounting. Under the equity method, the investment is initially recognized at cost, and the carrying amount is increased or decreased to recognize the investor&#8217;s share of the profit or loss of the investee after the date of acquisition. </P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>The Company accounted for the investments in its associates, under the equity method on the basis on the financial statements as of September 30, 2024, of <I>Gas Link S.A</I>. (&#8220;Link&#8221;) and <I>Transporte y Servicios de Gas en Uruguay S.A.</I> (Liquidated) (&#8220;TGU&#8221;). Accounting policies of associates have been changed where necessary to ensure consistency with the policies adopted by the Company. The Company&#8217;s management is not aware of any significant subsequent events which affected the financial statements as of September 30, 2024, of Link from this date to December 31, 2024. </P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>On December 26, 2024, the TGU board decided to dissolve and liquidate said company.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>Unrealized gains and losses resulting from transactions between <B>tgs</B> and the associate or joint venture are eliminated to the extent of the interest in the associate or joint venture. </P>

<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt;color:#0070C0'><B>4.b.3) Joint arrangement</B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>As indicated in &#8220;Note 23 &#8211;&nbsp;Associates and Joint Arrangement&#8221;, on August 7, 2017, the Company proceeded to create a UT (similar to a joint operation) <I>with SACDE Sociedad Argentina de Construcci&#243;n y Desarrollo Estrat&#233;gico S.A.</I> (&#8220;SACDE&#8221;) (&#8220;UT&#8221;). This operation is evaluated as a joint agreement under the provisions included &#8220;IFRS in Accounting standard 11 - Joint Arrangements&#8221; since the parties have joint control of the operation, meaning that the decisions of the relevant activities are taken under the unanimous consent of the parties.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>The Company has defined that the UT constitutes a joint operation given that it grants its participants a percentage of the rights over the assets and liabilities arising from each contract. Accordingly, the Company recognizes its share in the jointly operated assets, liabilities, revenues, costs and expenses.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>Accounting policies applicable to the UT have been modified and adapted, if applicable, to ensure consistency with the policies adopted by the Company. For further information regarding the UT, see Note 23.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'><B>4.c) Foreign currency translation</B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt;color:#0070C0'><B>4.c.1) Functional and presentation currency</B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>The consolidated financial statements are presented in thousands of Argentine Pesos, which is the Company&#8217;s functional currency restated to reflect the effects of inflation as indicated in Note 4.d). Each subsidiary or associate determines its own functional currency based on the currency of the primary economic environment in which these entities operate.</P>
<P style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'><B>4.c.2) Transactions and balances</B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>Foreign currency transactions are translated into the functional currency using the exchange rates at the dates of the transactions. Foreign exchange gains and losses resulting from the settlement of such transactions, and from the translation of monetary assets and liabilities denominated in foreign currencies at reporting date exchange rates, are recorded in the Statement of Comprehensive Income within financial income and financial expenses, as appropriate.</P>

<P align=center style='font:8pt Verdana;margin:0;text-indent:-1.15pt;margin-right:-35.5pt'>AS OF DECEMBER 31, 2024 AND COMPARATIVE INFORMATION </P>

<P align=center style='font:8pt Times New Roman;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt'>_____________________________________________________________________________________________________</P>
<HR style='border:0;height:0;width:0;margin:14pt 0 0 0'><P style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'><B>4.c.3) Associates</B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>The functional currency of the associate company TGU was the US dollar, because it was the currency in which it substantially generated its income and incurred its expenses. Assets and liabilities were converted into Argentine pesos using the exchange rate prevailing at the end of each year, their common stock and retained earnings at their historical exchange rates and results at average exchange rates.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>On December 26, 2024, said company was liquidated, so no balances are recorded for it as of December 31, 2024.</P>
<P style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'><B>4.d) Restatement to current currency - Comparative Information</B></P>
<P style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'><B>4.d.1) Regulatory framework</B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>The consolidated financial statements as of December 31, 2024, including comparative figures, have been restated to take into account changes in the general purchasing power of the Company&#8217;s functional currency (the Argentine peso) in accordance with IAS 29 &#8220;Financial Reporting in hyperinflationary economies&#8221; (&#8220;IAS 29&#8221;) and CNV General Resolution No. 777/2018. As a result, the financial statements are stated in terms of the current unit of measurement at the 2024 balance sheet date.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>IAS 29 requires that the financial statements of an entity that reports in the currency of a hyperinflationary economy, regardless of whether they are based on the historical cost method or the current cost method, are expressed in terms of the current unit of measurement at the closing date of the reporting period. In order to conclude on the existence of a hyperinflationary economy, the standard details a series of factors to be considered, among which is a cumulative inflation rate over three years that approaches or exceeds 100%.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>The accumulated inflation in three years is over 100%. Likewise, both the National Government projections and other available projections indicate that this trend will not be reversed in the short term.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>To evaluate the aforementioned quantitative condition, and also to restate the financial statements, the CNV has established that the series of indexes to be used for the application of IAS 29 is determined by the FACPCE. This series of indexes combines the National Consumer Price Index (&quot;CPI&quot;) as of January 2017 (base month: December 2016) with the Domestic Wholesale Price Index (&quot;WPI&quot;), both published by the Institute National Statistics and Census (&quot;INDEC&quot;) until that date. For the months of November and December 2015, for which there is no information from the INDEC on the evolution of the WPI, the variation in the CPI of the Autonomous City of Buenos Aires was applied.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>Considering the aforementioned index, inflation was 117.8%, 211.40% and 94.79% in the years ended December 31, 2024, 2023 and 2022 respectively.</P>
<P style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'><B>4.d.2) Restatement mechanism</B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>The financial statements must be adjusted to consider changes in the general purchasing power of the currency, so that they are expressed in the current unit of measurement at the end of the reporting period. Said requirements also include all the comparative information of the financial statements, without modifying the decisions made based on the financial information corresponding to those financial years.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>The figures as of December 31, 2023 and 2022, which are presented in these Consolidated Financial Statements for comparative purposes, arise from the restatement to the current unit of measure of the Financial Statements as of said dates, in accordance with IAS 29.</P>

<P align=center style='font:8pt Times New Roman;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt'>_____________________________________________________________________________________________________</P>
<HR style='border:0;height:0;width:0;margin:14pt 0 0 0'><P style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'><B><I>Restatement of the balance sheet</I></B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;text-indent:-7.05pt;margin-left:28.35pt'>i. Monetary items (those with a fixed nominal value in local currency) are not restated, since they are already expressed in the current unit of measurement at the closing date of the reporting period. In an inflationary period, maintaining monetary assets generates loss of purchasing power and maintaining monetary liabilities generates a gain in purchasing power, provided that such items are not subject to an adjustment mechanism that compensates to some extent for these effects. The monetary loss or gain is included in the result of the period in which it is reported.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;text-indent:-7.05pt;margin-left:28.35pt'>ii. The non-monetary items measured at their current values at the end of the reporting period are not restated for the purpose of their presentation in the balance sheet, but the adjustment process must be completed to determine in terms of a homogeneous unit of measurement the results produced by the holding of these non-monetary items.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;text-indent:-7.05pt;margin-left:28.35pt'>iii. The non-monetary items measured at historical cost or at a fair value as of a date prior to the closing date of the reporting period are restated by coefficients that reflect the variation in the general price level from the date of acquisition or revaluation to the closing date, proceeding then to compare the restated amounts of those assets with the corresponding recoverable values.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;text-indent:-7.05pt;margin-left:28.35pt'>iv. The restatement of non-monetary assets in the terms of the current unit of measurement at the end of the reporting period without an equivalent adjustment for tax purposes, results in a temporary taxable difference and the recognition of a deferred tax liability whose counterparty is recognized in the result of the period. For the closing of the subsequent period, the deferred tax items are restated for inflation to re-determine the charge to the result of the next period.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;text-indent:-7.05pt;margin-left:28.35pt'>v. When the capitalization of costs for loans in non-monetary assets in accordance with IAS 23 is applicable, the portion of those costs that compensate the lender for the effects of inflation is not capitalized.</P>
<P style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'><B><I>Restatement of the Comprehensive Income Statement</I></B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>Revenues and expenses (including interest and foreign exchange differences) are restated from the date of their booking, except for those items of the result that reflect or include in their determination the consumption of assets measured in purchasing power of a date before the consumption booked, which are restated based on the date of origin of the asset to which the item is related (for example, depreciation and other consumption of assets valued at historical cost); and also those results that arise from comparing two measurements expressed in purchasing power currency of different dates, for which it is necessary to identify the amounts compared, restate them separately, and make the comparison, but with the amounts already restated.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>The result of the exposure to the change in the purchasing power of the currency (monetary results) is presented in a separate line and reflects the effect of inflation on the monetary items.</P>
<P style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'><B><I>Restatement of the statement of changes in equity</I></B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>As of the transition date (January 1, 2016), the Company applied the following special rules:</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;text-indent:-7.05pt;margin-left:28.35pt'>i. The components of the capital stock were restated from the dates they were contributed.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;text-indent:-7.05pt;margin-left:28.35pt'>ii. Reserved earnings were maintained at the date of transition at their nominal value (legal amount without restatement).</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;text-indent:-7.05pt;margin-left:28.35pt'>iii. The restated unallocated results were determined by the difference between the net assets restated at the transition date and the rest of the initial equity components expressed as indicated in the preceding sections.</P>

<HR style='border:0;height:0;width:0;margin:14pt 0 0 0'><P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;text-indent:-7.05pt;margin-left:28.35pt'>iv. After the restatement at the transition date, all the components of the equity are restated by applying the general price index from the beginning of the period, and each variation of those components is restated from the date of contribution or from the moment in which is added by any other means.</P>
<P style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'><B><I>Restatement of the statement of cash flows</I></B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>IAS 29 requires that all items in this statement should be restated in terms of the current unit of measurement as of the closing date of the period for which it is reported.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>The monetary result generated by cash and cash equivalents is presented in the Statement of Cash Flows separately from cash flows from operating, investing and financing activities, as a specific item of the reconciliation between cash and cash equivalents at the beginning and at the end of the year.</P>
<P style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;text-indent:-22.7pt;margin-left:51.9pt;color:#0070C0'>&nbsp;</P>
<P style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'><B>4.e) Financial instruments</B></P>
<P style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'><B>4.e.1) Financial assets</B></P>
<P style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'><B>Recognition and initial measurement</B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>Financial assets are classified, at the time of initial recognition, as:</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt'><KBD style='position:absolute;text-align:right;margin-left:0pt;width:0pt;font:9pt Verdana'>i.</KBD><KBD style=margin-left:35.4pt></KBD>Financial assets subsequently measured at amortized cost, and&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt'>&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt'><KBD style='position:absolute;text-align:right;margin-left:0pt;width:0pt;font:9pt Verdana'>ii.</KBD><KBD style=margin-left:35.4pt></KBD>Financial assets subsequently measured at fair value (either with changes in other comprehensive income or with changes in results).&nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>The classification of financial assets at initial recognition depends on the financial asset&#8217;s contractual cash flow characteristics and the Company&#8217;s business model for managing them. For additional information, see Note 16.2.1.</P>
<P style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'><B>Subsequent measurement</B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>After initial recognition, financial assets are measured according to their initial classification according to the following categories:</P>
<P style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'><B><I>Financial assets measured at amortized cost </I></B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>It is the most relevant category used by the Company, financial assets are classified and measure at amortized cost if both of the following conditions are met:</P>
<P style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;text-indent:-18pt;margin-left:21.25pt'>The financial asset is held within a business model with the objective to hold financial assets in order to collect contractual cash flows; and</P>
<P style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:21.25pt'><KBD style='position:absolute;font:9pt Symbol;margin-left:0pt'>&#183;</KBD><KBD style=margin-left:14.15pt></KBD>The contractual terms of the financial asset give rise on specified dates to cash flows that are solely payments of principal and interest on the principal amount outstanding. &nbsp;</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>Financial assets measured at amortized cost are subsequently measured using the effective interest method. </P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>Gains and losses are recognized in the Statement of Comprehensive Income under financial results when the asset is derecognized, modified or impaired. </P>
<P style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'><B><I>Financial assets measured at fair value through OCI (Debt instruments)</I></B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>Corresponds to financial assets that are maintained in a business model whose objective is achieved by obtaining contractual cash flows and selling them.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>Unrealized gains or losses arising from changes in fair value are recognized as other comprehensive income, except for the accrual of interest, exchange rate difference and the </P>

<P align=center style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:0pt'>English translation of the original prepared in Spanish for publication in Argentina</P>

<P align=center style='font:8pt Times New Roman;margin:0;margin-right:-0.05pt'>_____________________________________________________________________________________________________</P>
<HR style='border:0;height:0;width:0;margin:14pt 0 0 0'><P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'><FONT style='font:9pt Verdana'>impairment of such assets that are recognized as financial results in the Statement of Comprehensive Income. At the time the asset is written off, the accumulated gain or loss is recognized as a financial result and it is eliminated from the respective reserve.</FONT></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>As of December 31, 2024 and 2023, the Company has not recognized any financial assets under this category.</P>
<P style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'><B><I>Financial assets designated at fair value through OCI (equity instruments) </I></B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>Upon initial recognition, the Company can elect to classify irrevocably its equity investments as equity instruments designated at fair value through OCI when they meet the definition of equity under IAS 32.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>Gains and losses on these financial assets are never recycled to profit or loss. Dividends are recognized as other income in the Statement of Comprehensive Income when the right of payment has been established, except when the Company benefits from such proceeds as a recovery of part of the cost of the financial asset, in which case, such gains are recorded in OCI. Equity instruments designated at fair value through OCI are not subject to impairment assessment.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>As of December 31, 2024 and 2023, the Company has not recognized any financial assets under this category.</P>
<P style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'><B><I>Financial assets measured at fair value through profit or loss </I></B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>In the event that financial assets are not classified according to the aforementioned categories, they will be subsequently measured at fair value, presenting gains or losses arising from changes in fair value in the income statement within financial results in the year in which they are originated.</P>
<P style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'><B>Impairment of financial assets </B></P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>The Company applies the Expected Credit loss (&#8220;ECL&#8221;) model for those financial assets accounted for at amortized cost or at fair value through OCI. The ECL is based on the difference between the contractual cash flows due in accordance with the contract and the cash flows that the Company expects to receive, discounted at an approximation of the original effective interest rate. To this end, the Company evaluates various factors, including credit risk, historical trends and other available information.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>The application of this model implies recognition of:</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;text-indent:-18pt;margin-left:54pt'>Expected credit losses within of 12 months: these are expected credit losses that result from possible default events within 12 months after the filing date; and</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;text-indent:-18pt;margin-left:54pt'>Expected credit losses during the life of the asset: these are expected credit losses that result from possible events of default during the expected life of a financial instrument.</P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>In case a loss allowance is recognized, the carrying amount of the asset is reduced through an impairment account and the amount of the loss is presented in the Statement of Comprehensive Income at the time it occurs. Subsequent recoveries of amounts previously written off are credited in the same line item. </P>
<P align=justify style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:10pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.4pt'>The impairment tests performed on accounts receivable are described in Note 4.h).</P>
<P style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'>&nbsp;</P>
<P style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'>&nbsp;</P>
<P style='font:9pt Verdana;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:10pt;margin-left:14.2pt;color:#0070C0'><B>4.e.2) Financial liabilities</B></P>
